The workshop has certain things in it that put me right back in mind of what happened, and with family gatherings, if my brother-in-law might be there, I tend to find a reason not to go. I know that probably sounds like I’m making too much of it, but I’ve gotten pretty practiced at planning around those situations, and I’m just tired of doing that.\n\nDoctor: What are these reminders that you're trying to avoid in your workshop and with your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly that the workshop itself has become one of those reminders, if that makes sense. Just like a picture that pops into my head. It can happen when I’m shaving or working on a painting, and for a second it’s like I’m right back there. My chest tightens up even now. I know that probably sounds odd after all this time.\n\nDoctor: What happened with your brother-in-law and the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure I can say it very well. I know that’s probably not the answer you’re looking for. It’s just the part that sticks with me.\n\nDoctor: Did the incident with your brother-in-law and the heavy tool involve physical harm to you?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I don’t want to say something that makes it sound bigger than it was, or put words to it wrong.\n\nWhat I can say is that it scared me badly, and that image of the tool is what stayed with me. Whether you’d call it physical harm… I don’t know. I guess I’m having trouble being definite about that.\n\nDoctor: Did your brother-in-law touch you with the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t honestly know if I can say it that clearly. I know that probably sounds odd after all this time.\n\nDoctor: What happened with your brother-in-law and the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure I can say it very well. I don’t want to accuse anybody unfairly, you know?\n\nWhat I remember most clearly is his hand and that heavy tool. The rest gets harder to put into words. I know that’s probably not the answer you’re looking for. It’s just the part that sticks with me.\n\nDoctor: Did the incident with your brother-in-law and the heavy tool involve physical harm to you?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I don’t want to say something that makes it sound bigger than it was, or put words to it wrong.\n\nWhat I can say is that it scared me badly, and that image of the tool is what stayed with me. Whether you’d call it physical harm… I don’t know. I guess I’m having trouble being definite about that.\n\nDoctor: Did your brother-in-law touch you with the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t honestly know if I can say it that clearly. I remember him having it, and I remember being scared of it, but whether it actually touched me… that part isn’t solid in my mind.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that’s not very helpful. I just don’t want to give you an answer that isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were in danger of being hurt by your brother-in-law when you saw him with the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not completely sure. I mean, I was scared, yes, but I don’t know if I can fairly say I knew I was going to be hurt.\n\nIt felt threatening in the moment, seeing him with it, but I don’t want to make it sound like more than I can honestly remember. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think what happened with your brother-in-law could be considered a traumatic event for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor. Maybe… maybe it could be, but I’m not sure I should call it that.\n\nI mean, it bothered me, and it’s stayed with me some, but I don’t want to make too much of it if it doesn’t really fit. I guess I’d rather have you tell me whether that’s the right word for it.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been experiencing these reminders and avoiding certain situations?\n\nPatient: It’s been… well, I guess since that confrontation with my brother-in-law. That was about two years and four months ago now.\n\nI don’t mean to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s when I started noticing those reminders and kind of steering clear of certain things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding other places or activities besides your workshop and family gatherings because of these reminders?\n\nPatient: Not too many, I don’t think. Mostly it’s my workshop, and then family get-togethers where there’s a chance he’ll be there.\n\nI came in because I realized I’m still kind of planning around those things after all this time, you know? It’s not that I can’t manage, exactly, but I’ve been avoiding my workshop, and I skip family gatherings if there’s a chance my brother-in-law will be there. After all these years, it feels a little silly to say out loud, but those things bring up reminders I’d rather not run into. So I thought maybe I should finally talk to someone about it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what specifically you're trying to avoid when you skip family gatherings or don't go to your workshop?\n\nPatient: Well… mostly it’s the reminders, I guess. The workshop has certain things in it that put me right back in mind of what happened, and with family gatherings, if my brother-in-law might be there, I tend to find a reason not to go. I know that probably sounds like I’m making too much of it, but I’ve gotten pretty practiced at planning around those situations, and I’m just tired of doing that.\n\nDoctor: What are these reminders that you're trying to avoid in your workshop and with your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly that the workshop itself has become one of those reminders, if that makes sense. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic. I just find myself steering clear of going in there, and then with family gatherings, if I hear my brother-in-law might be there, I’ll usually come up with some reason not to go.\n\nI guess I’m tired of planning everything around avoiding those things. I know it’s been a long time, but they still bring it up for me.\n\nDoctor: What happened that you're being reminded of?\n\nPatient: It was… well, it’s a little hard to say plainly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, or like I’m being dramatic.\n\nBut sometimes, out of nowhere, I’ll see his hand again with that heavy tool in it. Just like a picture that pops into my head. It can happen when I’m shaving or working on a painting, and for a second it’s like I’m right back there. My chest tightens up even now. I know that probably sounds odd after all this time.\n\nDoctor: What happened with your brother-in-law and the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure I can say it very well. I don’t want to accuse anybody unfairly, you know?\n\nWhat I remember most clearly is his hand and that heavy tool. The rest gets harder to put into words. I know that’s probably not the answer you’re looking for. It’s just the part that sticks with me.\n\nDoctor: Did the incident with your brother-in-law and the heavy tool involve physical harm to you?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I don’t want to say something that makes it sound bigger than it was, or put words to it wrong.\n\nWhat I can say is that it scared me badly, and that image of the tool is what stayed with me. Whether you’d call it physical harm… I don’t know. I guess I’m having trouble being definite about that.\n\nDoctor: Did your brother-in-law touch you with the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t honestly know if I can say it that clearly. I remember him having it, and I remember being scared of it, but whether it actually touched me… that part isn’t solid in my mind.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that’s not very helpful. I just don’t want to give you an answer that isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were in danger of being hurt by your brother-in-law when you saw him with the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not completely sure. I mean, I was scared, yes, but I don’t know if I can fairly say I knew I was going to be hurt.\n\nIt felt threatening in the moment, seeing him with it, but I don’t want to make it sound like more than I can honestly remember. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think what happened with your brother-in-law could be considered a traumatic event for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor. Maybe… maybe it could be, but I’m not sure I should call it that.\n\nI mean, it bothered me, and it’s stayed with me some, but I don’t want to make too much of it if it doesn’t really fit. I guess I’d rather have you tell me whether that’s the right word for it.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been experiencing these reminders and avoiding certain situations?\n\nPatient: It’s been… well, I guess since that confrontation with my brother-in-law. That was about two years and four months ago now.\n\nI don’t mean to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s when I started noticing those reminders and kind of steering clear of certain things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding other places or activities besides your workshop and family gatherings because of these reminders?\n\nPatient: Not too many, I don’t think. Mostly it’s my workshop, and then family get-togethers where there’s a chance he’ll be there.\n\nI came in because I realized I’m still kind of planning around those things after all this time, you know? I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I’m tired of arranging my life just so I don’t run into reminders of it.\n\nDoctor: Are there people, aside from your brother-in-law, that you've been avoiding because of what happened?\n\nPatient: Mostly just him, I think. I don’t really avoid other people exactly… except maybe if a family get-together means he might be there, then I tend to stay away from the whole thing.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m avoiding more than one person, but it’s really more about not running into him or being reminded of it. That’s part of why I came in, I guess—I’m tired of planning around all that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I was scared, yes, but I don’t know if I can fairly say I knew I was going to be hurt.\n\nIt felt threatening in the moment, seeing him with it, but I don’t want to make it sound like more than I can honestly remember. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think what happened with your brother-in-law could be considered a traumatic event for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor. Maybe… maybe it could be, but I’m not sure I should call it that.\n\nI mean, it bothered me, and it’s stayed with me some, but I don’t want to make too much of it if it doesn’t really fit. I guess I’d rather have you tell me whether that’s the right word for it.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been experiencing these reminders and avoiding certain situations?\n\nPatient: It’s been… well, I guess since that confrontation with my brother-in-law. That was about two years and four months ago now.\n\nI don’t mean to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s when I started noticing those reminders and kind of steering clear of certain things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding other places or activities besides your workshop and family gatherings because of these reminders?\n\nPatient: Not too many, I don’t think. Mostly it’s my workshop, and then family get-togethers where there’s a chance he’ll be there.\n\nI came in because I realized I’m still kind of planning around those things after all this time, you know? I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I’m tired of arranging my life just so I don’t run into reminders of it.\n\nDoctor: Are there people, aside from your brother-in-law, that you've been avoiding because of what happened?\n\nPatient: Mostly just him, I think. I don’t really avoid other people exactly… except maybe if a family get-together means he might be there, then I tend to stay away from the whole thing.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m avoiding more than one person, but it’s really more about not running into him or being reminded of it. That’s part of why I came in, I guess—I’m tired of planning around all that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself reliving the moment when you saw your brother-in-law with the heavy tool, feeling like you're back in that situation again?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not like I’m actually there, exactly. But out of nowhere I’ll see his hand with that heavy tool again, just like a picture that pops into my head.\n\nIt can happen when I’m doing something ordinary, like shaving or working on a painting. I try not to make too much of it, but it still makes my chest tighten a bit. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s not that I can’t manage, exactly, but I’ve been avoiding my workshop, and I skip family gatherings if there’s a chance my brother-in-law will be there. After all these years, it feels a little silly to say out loud, but those things bring up reminders I’d rather not run into. So I thought maybe I should finally talk to someone about it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what specifically you're trying to avoid when you skip family gatherings or don't go to your workshop?\n\nPatient: Well… mostly it’s the reminders, I guess. The workshop has certain things in it that put me right back in mind of what happened, and with family gatherings, if my brother-in-law might be there, I tend to find a reason not to go. I know that probably sounds like I’m making too much of it, but I’ve gotten pretty practiced at planning around those situations, and I’m just tired of doing that.\n\nDoctor: What are these reminders that you're trying to avoid in your workshop and with your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly that the workshop itself has become one of those reminders, if that makes sense. I know it’s been a long time, but they still bring it up for me.\n\nDoctor: What happened that you're being reminded of?\n\nPatient: It was… well, it’s a little hard to say plainly. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I’ve just gotten tired of… arranging my life around certain things. It’s not that I can’t manage, exactly, but I’ve been avoiding my workshop, and I skip family gatherings if there’s a chance my brother-in-law will be there. After all these years, it feels a little silly to say out loud, but those things bring up reminders I’d rather not run into. So I thought maybe I should finally talk to someone about it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what specifically you're trying to avoid when you skip family gatherings or don't go to your workshop?\n\nPatient: Well… mostly it’s the reminders, I guess. The workshop has certain things in it that put me right back in mind of what happened, and with family gatherings, if my brother-in-law might be there, I tend to find a reason not to go. I know that probably sounds like I’m making too much of it, but I’ve gotten pretty practiced at planning around those situations, and I’m just tired of doing that.\n\nDoctor: What are these reminders that you're trying to avoid in your workshop and with your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly that the workshop itself has become one of those reminders, if that makes sense. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic. I just find myself steering clear of going in there, and then with family gatherings, if I hear my brother-in-law might be there, I’ll usually come up with some reason not to go.\n\nI guess I’m tired of planning everything around avoiding those things. I know it’s been a long time, but they still bring it up for me.\n\nDoctor: What happened that you're being reminded of?\n\nPatient: It was… well, it’s a little hard to say plainly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, or like I’m being dramatic.\n\nBut sometimes, out of nowhere, I’ll see his hand again with that heavy tool in it. Just like a picture that pops into my head. It can happen when I’m shaving or working on a painting, and for a second it’s like I’m right back there. My chest tightens up even now. I know that probably sounds odd after all this time.\n\nDoctor: What happened with your brother-in-law and the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure I can say it very well. I don’t want to accuse anybody unfairly, you know?\n\nWhat I remember most clearly is his hand and that heavy tool. The rest gets harder to put into words. I know that’s probably not the answer you’re looking for. It’s just the part that sticks with me.\n\nDoctor: Did the incident with your brother-in-law and the heavy tool involve physical harm to you?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I don’t want to say something that makes it sound bigger than it was, or put words to it wrong.\n\nWhat I can say is that it scared me badly, and that image of the tool is what stayed with me. Whether you’d call it physical harm… I don’t know. I guess I’m having trouble being definite about that.\n\nDoctor: Did your brother-in-law touch you with the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t honestly know if I can say it that clearly. I remember him having it, and I remember being scared of it, but whether it actually touched me… that part isn’t solid in my mind.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that’s not very helpful. I just don’t want to give you an answer that isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were in danger of being hurt by your brother-in-law when you saw him with the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not completely sure. I mean, I was scared, yes, but I don’t know if I can fairly say I knew I was going to be hurt.\n\nIt felt threatening in the moment, seeing him with it, but I don’t want to make it sound like more than I can honestly remember. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think what happened with your brother-in-law could be considered a traumatic event for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I’ve just gotten tired of… arranging my life around certain things. It’s not that I can’t manage, exactly, but I’ve been avoiding my workshop, and I skip family gatherings if there’s a chance my brother-in-law will be there. After all these years, it feels a little silly to say out loud, but those things bring up reminders I’d rather not run into. So I thought maybe I should finally talk to someone about it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what specifically you're trying to avoid when you skip family gatherings or don't go to your workshop?\n\nPatient: Well… mostly it’s the reminders, I guess. The workshop has certain things in it that put me right back in mind of what happened, and with family gatherings, if my brother-in-law might be there, I tend to find a reason not to go. I know that probably sounds like I’m making too much of it, but I’ve gotten pretty practiced at planning around those situations, and I’m just tired of doing that.\n\nDoctor: What are these reminders that you're trying to avoid in your workshop and with your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly that the workshop itself has become one of those reminders, if that makes sense. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic. I just find myself steering clear of going in there, and then with family gatherings, if I hear my brother-in-law might be there, I’ll usually come up with some reason not to go.\n\nI guess I’m tired of planning everything around avoiding those things. I know it’s been a long time, but they still bring it up for me.\n\nDoctor: What happened that you're being reminded of?\n\nPatient: It was… well, it’s a little hard to say plainly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, or like I’m being dramatic.\n\nBut sometimes, out of nowhere, I’ll see his hand again with that heavy tool in it. Just like a picture that pops into my head. It can happen when I’m shaving or working on a painting, and for a second it’s like I’m right back there. My chest tightens up even now. I know that probably sounds odd after all this time.\n\nDoctor: What happened with your brother-in-law and the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure I can say it very well. I don’t want to accuse anybody unfairly, you know?\n\nWhat I remember most clearly is his hand and that heavy tool. The rest gets harder to put into words. I know that’s probably not the answer you’re looking for. It’s just the part that sticks with me.\n\nDoctor: Did the incident with your brother-in-law and the heavy tool involve physical harm to you?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I just don’t want to give you an answer that isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were in danger of being hurt by your brother-in-law when you saw him with the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not completely sure. I mean, I was scared, yes, but I don’t know if I can fairly say I knew I was going to be hurt.\n\nIt felt threatening in the moment, seeing him with it, but I don’t want to make it sound like more than I can honestly remember. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, or like I’m being dramatic.\n\nBut sometimes, out of nowhere, I’ll see his hand again with that heavy tool in it. Just like a picture that pops into my head. It can happen when I’m shaving or working on a painting, and for a second it’s like I’m right back there. My chest tightens up even now. I know that probably sounds odd after all this time.\n\nDoctor: What happened with your brother-in-law and the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure I can say it very well. I don’t want to accuse anybody unfairly, you know?\n\nWhat I remember most clearly is his hand and that heavy tool. The rest gets harder to put into words. I know that’s probably not the answer you’re looking for. It’s just the part that sticks with me.\n\nDoctor: Did the incident with your brother-in-law and the heavy tool involve physical harm to you?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I don’t want to say something that makes it sound bigger than it was, or put words to it wrong.\n\nWhat I can say is that it scared me badly, and that image of the tool is what stayed with me. Whether you’d call it physical harm… I don’t know. I guess I’m having trouble being definite about that.\n\nDoctor: Did your brother-in-law touch you with the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t honestly know if I can say it that clearly. I remember him having it, and I remember being scared of it, but whether it actually touched me… that part isn’t solid in my mind.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that’s not very helpful. I just don’t want to give you an answer that isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were in danger of being hurt by your brother-in-law when you saw him with the heavy tool?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not completely sure. I mean, I was scared, yes, but I don’t know if I can fairly say I knew I was going to be hurt.\n\nIt felt threatening in the moment, seeing him with it, but I don’t want to make it sound like more than I can honestly remember. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think what happened with your brother-in-law could be considered a traumatic event for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor. Maybe… maybe it could be, but I’m not sure I should call it that.\n\nI mean, it bothered me, and it’s stayed with me some, but I don’t want to make too much of it if it doesn’t really fit. I guess I’d rather have you tell me whether that’s the right word for it.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been experiencing these reminders and avoiding certain situations?\n\nPatient: It’s been… well, I guess since that confrontation with my brother-in-law. That was about two years and four months ago now.\n\nI don’t mean to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s when I started noticing those reminders and kind of steering clear of certain things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding other places or activities besides your workshop and family gatherings because of these reminders?\n\nPatient: Not too many, I don’t think. Mostly it’s my workshop, and then family get-togethers where there’s a chance he’ll be there.\n\nI came in because I realized I’m still kind of planning around those things after all this time, you know? I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I’m tired of arranging my life just so I don’t run into reminders of it.\n\nDoctor: Are there people, aside from your brother-in-law, that you've been avoiding because of what happened?\n\nPatient: Mostly just him, I think. I don’t really avoid other people exactly… except maybe if a family get-together means he might be there, then I tend to stay away from the whole thing.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m avoiding more than one person, but it’s really more about not running into him or being reminded of it. That’s part of why I came in, I guess—I’m tired of planning around all that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself reliving the moment when you saw your brother-in-law with the heavy tool, feeling like you're back in that situation again?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not like I’m actually there, exactly. But out of nowhere I’ll see his hand with that heavy tool again, just like a picture that pops into my head.\n\nIt can happen when I’m doing something ordinary, like shaving or working on a painting. I try not to make too much of it, but it still makes my chest tighten a bit. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're actually back in the situation with your brother-in-law, reliving it as if it's happening again?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like I’m back there, no. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nIt’s more that, out of nowhere, I’ll see his hand with that heavy tool again, almost like a still picture in my mind. It can happen when I’m shaving or when I’m working on a painting, and my chest tightens a bit. But I still know where I am, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the moment with your brother-in-law as if it's actually happening again, not just seeing a picture in your mind?\n\nPatient: No… not like it’s actually happening again, I don’t think. I’m aware I’m here.\n\nIt’s more that, out of nowhere, I’ll see his hand with that heavy tool again, like a picture popping into my mind. Sometimes when I’m shaving or working on a painting. It’s quick, but it still makes my chest tighten a little. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other people, besides your brother-in-law, that you avoid because they remind you of what happened?\n\nPatient: Not really other people, no… I mean, it’s mostly him.\n\nI do find myself steering clear of family get-togethers if I think he might be there. And I’ve been avoiding my own workshop some too, which sounds a little silly, I know, but it reminds me of it. After all this time, I guess I’m tired of planning around not running into those reminders. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any people, aside from your brother-in-law, that you avoid seeing or interacting with because they remind you of what happened?\n\nPatient: No, not really anybody else specifically. It’s more… if there’s a family get-together where he might be there, I’ll find a reason not to go.\n\nAnd I’ve been steering clear of my workshop some too, which I know isn’t a person, but it brings it all back enough that I avoid it. That’s honestly part of why I came in—I’m tired of planning around reminders like that, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any people, aside from your brother-in-law, that you avoid seeing because they remind you of what happened?\n\nPatient: Aside from him, no… I don’t think there’s anyone else I avoid seeing, not specifically.\n\nIt’s more that I’ll skip family things if I think he might be there. And, well, I’ve been staying away from my workshop some too, even though that’s not a person. That’s part of why I came in, I guess—I’m tired of planning around reminders after all this time. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
